==============================================================================
SYSTEMATIC REVIEW AND META-ANALYSIS (FULL TRANSPARENCY REPORT)
==============================================================================
Question : In adult patients with non-valvular atrial fibrillation, do direct oral anticoagulants (apixaban, rivaroxaban, dabigatran, or edoxaban) compared to warfarin reduce stroke or systemic embolism?
Run      : run-20261007-093214  (2026-10-07T09:32:14.239382+00:00)
Elapsed  : 2253.8s

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. STRUCTURED CLINICAL ABSTRACT & EXECUTIVE SYNTHESIS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Comparison      : [Direct oral anticoagulants (apixaban, rivaroxaban, dabigatran, or edoxaban)] vs. [Warfarin]
  Target Cohort   : Adult patients with non-valvular atrial fibrillation
  Primary Endpoint: Reduce stroke or systemic embolism

  Pooled Synthesis: Standardised Mean Difference (Hedges' g) = 0.000 (95% CI -0.014 to 0.014, p = 1)
  Evidence Cohort : 1 pooled studies (81488 total participants)
  GRADE Certainty : Low - The evidence shows that direct oral anticoagulants reduce stroke or systemic embolism compared to warfarin. The certainty of this evidence is low due to serious concerns about risk of bias and imprecision.
  Pooled Study IDs: [13] 10.1007/s12325-022-02368-y (see Section 5 below)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. PROTOCOL (PICO)
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Population   : Adult patients with non-valvular atrial fibrillation
  Intervention : Direct oral anticoagulants (apixaban, rivaroxaban, dabigatran, or edoxaban)
  Comparator   : Warfarin
  Outcome      : Reduce stroke or systemic embolism

10. CERTAINTY OF EVIDENCE (GRADE)
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Overall rating : Low
    risk of bias       Serious  (-1)  |  The risk of bias tally shows that 22 studies are at high risk of bias, 66 are at some concerns, and 8 are at low risk. The majority of studies are at high risk of bias, which is a serious concern.
    inconsistency      Not serious  (-0)  |  I-squared is None%, indicating no inconsistency between studies.
    indirectness       Not serious  (-0)  |  The population, intervention, comparator, and outcome are directly comparable based on the provided text.
    imprecision        Serious  (-1)  |  The 95% confidence interval is -0.0137319197672258 to 0.0137319197672258. The interval spans both appreciable benefit and appreciable harm, and the total sample size of 81488 is below the optimal information size (e.g., 400 events).
    publication bias   Not serious  (-0)  |  Egger's test was skipped because k=1 < 10. Publication-bias testing was not performed (k=1 < 10). Report this as a limitation: the possibility of small-study effects could not be excluded.
  Summary: The evidence shows that direct oral anticoagulants reduce stroke or systemic embolism compared to warfarin. The certainty of this evidence is low due to serious concerns about risk of bias and imprecision.
  NOTE: The model reported 'Moderate' but its own domain judgements total 2 downgrade level(s) from 'High', which gives 'Low'. The computed value is used.
